\oldpage[IV-4]{86}

  \item[\textup{c$'$)}] $h$ is smooth at the point $y$ and there exists an open neighbourhood $U$ of $y$ in $Y$ such that $j|U:U\to X$ is a quasi-regular immersion (in other words (16.9.8), there exists a neighbourhood $U$ of $y$ in $Y$ in which $\sh{N}_{Y/X}$ is a locally free $\sh{O}_Y$-Module and the canonical homomorphism
  \[
  \mathbf{S}^{\bullet}_{\sh{O}_Y}(\sh{N}_{Y/X})\to\mathscr{G}r^{\bullet}(j)
  \]
  is bijective).
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

To prove the equivalence of a) and b), we may restrict to the case where $S=\Spec(A)$ and $X=\Spec(B)$ are affine, with $Y=\Spec(B/\mathfrak J)$, where $\mathfrak J$ is an ideal of finite type of $B$, and $B$ is a smooth $A$-algebra (17.3.2, (ii)). It then suffices to apply the Jacobian criterion (\textbf{0}, 22.6.1) as well as (\textbf{0}, 19.1.14), taking into account the fact that $\varOmega^1_{X/S}$ is a locally free $\sh{O}_X$-Module in a neighbourhood of $x$ and $\sh{N}_{Y/X}$ an $\sh{O}_Y$-Module of finite type (16.1.6).

Secondly, let us prove that a) implies c'). We may again restrict to the case where $S$, $X$, $Y$ are affine, $B$ and $B/\mathfrak J$ smooth $A$-algebras, and it then follows from (17.7.9) and (8.10.5, (iv)) that there exist a Noetherian subring $A'$ of $A$, an $A'$-algebra of finite type $B'$ and an ideal $\mathfrak J'$ of $B'$ such that $B=B'\otimes_{A'}A$, $\mathfrak J=\mathfrak J'B$ and $B'$ and $B'/\mathfrak J'$ are smooth $A'$-algebras. Note that, by (\textbf{0}, 19.3.8), $B'$ is again a formally smooth $A'$-algebra when $A'$ is given the discrete topology and $B'$ the $\mathfrak J'$-preadic topology. Consequently (\textbf{0}, 19.5.4), $\mathfrak J'/\mathfrak J'^2$ is a projective $(B'/\mathfrak J')$-module and the canonical homomorphism
\[
\mathbf{S}^{\bullet}_{B'/\mathfrak J'}(\mathfrak J'/\mathfrak J'^2)\to\operatorname{gr}_{\mathfrak J'}^{\bullet}(B')
\]
is bijective. In other words (16.9.8), the immersion $\Spec(B'/\mathfrak J')\to\Spec(B')$ is quasi-regular, hence regular since $B'$ is Noetherian (16.9.10). But since by hypothesis $B'/\mathfrak J'$ is a flat $A'$-module (17.5.1) and $B'$ an $A'$-algebra of finite presentation, we may apply (11.3.8), replacing $\sh{F}$ by $\widetilde{\mathfrak J}'$, and we therefore see (by base change) that $j$ is a regular immersion, and \emph{a fortiori} quasi-regular. The fact that $B$ is an $A$-algebra of finite presentation and a flat $A$-module shows moreover, by virtue of (11.3.8), that conditions c') and c) are equivalent, and are stable under base change.

Let us finally show that c) implies a). From the fact that, in (11.3.8), condition b) implies c), we already see that if c) is satisfied, $f$ is \emph{flat} at the point $x$; by virtue of (17.5.1), everything therefore comes down to seeing that under hypothesis \emph{c)}, $f^{-1}(s)$ is smooth over $\boldsymbol{k}(s)$ at the point $x$, setting $s=f(x)$; since we have remarked that condition \emph{c)} is stable under base change, we see that we are reduced to the case where $S=\Spec(k)$ is the spectrum of a field, and taking into account (17.7.1, (ii)) we may suppose that $k$ is \emph{algebraically closed}. It then suffices to prove that $\sh{O}_{X,x}$ is a \emph{regular} ring (17.5.1). Now, by hypothesis we have $\sh{O}_{Y,y}=\sh{O}_{X,x}/\sh{J}_x$, where $\sh{J}_x$ is an ideal generated by an $\sh{O}_{X,x}$-regular sequence $(t_i)$, and $\sh{O}_{Y,y}$ is a regular ring; since the $t_i$ form part of a system of parameters for $\sh{O}_{X,x}$ (\textbf{0}, 16.4.1), the conclusion follows from (\textbf{0}, 17.1.7).

\begin{corollary}[17.12.2]
\label{IV.17.12.2}
With the notation of (17.12.1), suppose that $f$ is smooth at the point $x$, and let $Y$ be a closed sub-prescheme of $X$ defined by an ideal $\sh{J}$ of $\sh{O}_X$, and $S$-smooth at the point $x$. Let $(g_i)_{1\leq i\leq r}$ be sections of $\sh{J}$ over $X$. The following conditions are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}
  \item[\textup{a)}] The $(g_i)_x$ form a system of generators of the $\sh{O}_{X,x}$-module $\sh{J}_x$ whose number of elements is the smallest possible.
  \item[\textup{b)}] If $g_i'$ is the canonical image of $g_i$ in $\Gamma(X,\sh{J}/\sh{J}^2)$, the $(g_i')_x$ form a basis of the $(\sh{O}_{X,x}/\sh{J}_x)$-module $\sh{J}_x/\sh{J}_x^2$.
----- English, ega4/ega4-17.tex, page IV-4 87 -----
\oldpage[IV-4]{87}

  \item[\textup{c)}] The images of the $d_{X/S}g_i$ in $(\varOmega^1_{X/S})_x\otimes_{\sh{O}_{X,x}}\boldsymbol{k}(x)$ are elements linearly independent over $\boldsymbol{k}(x)$, and the $(g_i)_x$ generate $\sh{J}_x$.
  \item[\textup{d)}] There exist an open neighbourhood $U$ of $x$ in $X$ and sections $g_j$ ($r+1\leq j\leq n$) of $\sh{O}_X$ over $U$ such that the $S$-morphism $u:U\to S[T_1,\ldots,T_n]$ corresponding to the homomorphism $\sh{O}_S^n\to f_*(\sh{O}_U)$ defined by the sections $g_i|U$ \textup{($1\leq i\leq r$)} and $g_j$ ($r+1\leq j\leq n$) \textup{(\textbf{II}, 1.2.7)} is an étale morphism, and the inverse image of the sub-prescheme $Y'=S[T_{r+1},\ldots,T_n]$ under this morphism is the prescheme induced by $j(Y)$ on the open subset $U\cap j(Y)$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{corollary}

Since $(g_i')_x$ is the canonical image of $(g_i)_x$, the equivalence of a) and b) follows from Nakayama's lemma, $\sh{J}_x$ being of finite type and $\sh{J}_x/\sh{J}_x^2$ a free $(\sh{O}_{X,x}/\sh{J}_x)$-module (17.10.4) (Bourbaki, \emph{Alg. comm.}, chap. II, § 3, n\textsuperscript{o} 2, prop. 5). By virtue of (17.12.1, \emph{b)}), $\sh{J}_x/\sh{J}_x^2$ is canonically identified with a direct factor of the free $(\sh{O}_{X,x}/\sh{J}_x)$-module of rank $n$, $(\varOmega^1_{X/S})_x\otimes_{\sh{O}_{X,x}}(\sh{O}_{X,x}/\sh{J}_x)$, and the equivalence of b) and c) follows from Bourbaki, \emph{loc. cit.} Moreover, if a) is satisfied, $(g_i')_x$ is thus identified with $(d_{X/S}g_i)_x\otimes1$ for $1\leq i\leq r$; the $\sh{O}_{X,x}$-module $(\varOmega^1_{X/S})_x$ being free of rank $n$, it again follows from Bourbaki, \emph{loc. cit.}, that, replacing $X$ if necessary by an open neighbourhood of $x$, we may suppose that there exist $n\setmin r$ sections $g_i$ ($r+1\leq i\leq n$) of $\sh{O}_X$ over $X$ such that the $(d_{X/S}g_i)_x$ for $1\leq i\leq n$ form a basis of $(\varOmega^1_{X/S})_x$; the fact that the corresponding morphism $u$ is étale at the point $x$ then follows from (17.11.3); for the same reason, the morphism $u^{-1}(Y')\to Y'$, restriction of $u$, is étale at the point $x$; replacing $X$ by a neighbourhood of $x$, we may suppose these two morphisms étale. Moreover, it is immediate that $Y$ (identified, for simplicity, with the closed sub-prescheme $j(Y)$ of $X$) is a closed sub-prescheme of $u^{-1}(Y')$, and by virtue of the choice of the $g_i$ for $i>r$ and of (17.2.5) and (17.11.2), the restriction of $u$ to $Y$ may also be supposed étale. We deduce that for every $y'\in Y'$, the immersion $Y\cap u^{-1}(y')\to u^{-1}(y')$ is open, from which we conclude with the help of (17.9.6) that $Y\to u^{-1}(Y')$ is an open immersion; this proves that a) implies d). The converse follows immediately from (17.11.3).

\begin{corollary}[17.12.3]
\label{IV.17.12.3}
Let $f:X\to S$ be a morphism locally of finite presentation, $u:S\to X$ an $S$-section of $X$. For $f$ to be smooth at a point $x\in X$, it is necessary and sufficient that $u$ be a quasi-regular immersion in a neighbourhood of $s=f(x)$.
\end{corollary}

The conclusion follows from (17.12.1), since $f\circ u=1_S$ is smooth.

\begin{proposition}[17.12.4]
\label{IV.17.12.4}
Every smooth morphism $f:X\to S$ is differentially smooth (in other words (16.10.5) $\Delta_f:X\to X\times_SX$ is a quasi-regular immersion). \emph{In particular, the $\sh{P}_f^n=\sh{P}_{X/S}^n$ and the $\operatorname{gr}_n(\sh{P}_{X/S})$ are locally free $\sh{O}_X$-Modules of finite type.}
\end{proposition}

It suffices to remark that the structural morphism $p_1:X\times_SX\to X$ is smooth (17.3.3, (iii)) and that $\Delta_f$ is an $X$-section for $p_1$; the conclusion follows from (17.12.3).

\begin{proposition}[17.12.5]
\label{IV.17.12.5}
Let $f:X\to Y$ be a morphism locally of finite presentation. The following conditions are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}
  \item[\textup{a)}] $f$ is differentially smooth.
  \item[\textup{b)}] For every morphism $g:Y'\to Y$, if we set $X'=X\times_Y Y'$ and $f'=f_{(Y')}:X'\to Y'$, then, for every $Y'$-section $s'$ of $X'$, $f'$ is smooth at all the points of $s'(Y')$.
  \item[\textup{c)}] The second projection $p_2:X\times_YX\to X$ is smooth at all the points of the diagonal $\Delta_f(X)$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}
----- English, ega4/ega4-17.tex, page IV-4 88 -----
\oldpage[IV-4]{88}

Condition c) is a particular case of b): indeed it suffices to take $Y'=X$ and $g=f$ in b), since then $f'$ is none other than the second projection $p_2$, and $\Delta_f$ is an $X$-section of $X\times_YX$. Secondly, c) implies a), since $p_2$ is a morphism locally of finite presentation, and if $p_2$ is smooth at the points of $\Delta_f(X)$, $\Delta_f$ is a quasi-regular immersion (17.12.3), hence $f$ is differentially smooth. Finally, let us show that a) implies b); if $\Delta_f$ is a quasi-regular immersion, $p_2$ is smooth at all the points of $\Delta_f(X)$ (17.12.3).

Now, if $g':X'\to X$ is the canonical projection, and $v=(g',h\circ f')_Y:X'\to X\times_YX$, we verify immediately that the diagram
\[
\xymatrix{
  X\times_Y X \ar[d]_{p_2} & X' \ar[l]_-v \ar[d]^{f'}\\
  X & Y' \ar[l]^{h=g'\circ s'}
}
\]
is commutative and identifies $X'$ with the product $(X\times_YX)\times_XY'$ (\textbf{I}, 3.3.9); taking into account the fact that the diagram (17.4.1.1) identifies $Y'$ with the product of the $(X\times_YX)$-preschemes $X$ and $X'$, we conclude, from the fact that $p_2$ is smooth at every point of $\Delta_f(X)$, that $f'$ is smooth at every point of $s'(Y')$ (17.3.3, (iii)).

\begin{corollary}[17.12.6]
\label{IV.17.12.6}
The notation being that of \textup{(8.8.1)}, suppose $X_\alpha$ quasi-compact, and $f_\alpha:X_\alpha\to S_\alpha$ locally of finite presentation. For $f:X\to S$ to be differentially smooth, it is necessary and sufficient that there exist $\lambda\geq\alpha$ such that $f_\lambda:X_\lambda\to S_\lambda$ is differentially smooth (in which case $f_\mu:X_\mu\to S_\mu$ is differentially smooth for $\mu\geq\lambda$).
\end{corollary}

The sufficiency of the condition and the last assertion follow from (16.10.4). To prove that the condition is necessary, note that $\Delta_f:X\to X\times_SX$ and $p_2:X\times_SX\to X$ are deduced by base change from $\Delta_{f_\lambda}:X_\lambda\to X_\lambda\times_{S_\lambda}X_\lambda$ and $p_{2,\lambda}:X_\lambda\times_{S_\lambda}X_\lambda\to X_\lambda$. By virtue of (17.12.5), for every $z\in\Delta_f(X)$, there exists an affine open neighbourhood $U(z)$ of $z$ in $X\times_SX$ such that $p_2$ is smooth in $U(z)$; by virtue of (8.2.11) and (17.7.8), there exist an index $\lambda(z)$ and a neighbourhood $U_{\lambda(z)}$ of the projection of $z$ in $X_{\lambda(z)}\times_{S_{\lambda(z)}}X_{\lambda(z)}$ such that $U(z)$ is the inverse image of $U_{\lambda(z)}$ and $p_{2,\lambda(z)}$ is smooth in $U_{\lambda(z)}$. Since $X$ is quasi-compact, we can cover $\Delta_f(X)$ by finitely many neighbourhoods $U(z_i)$; by virtue of (8.3.4), there exists a $\lambda$ greater than all the $\lambda(z_i)$ such that the inverse images of the $U_{\lambda(z_i)}$ in $X_\lambda\times_{S_\lambda}X_\lambda$ form a covering of $\Delta_{f_\lambda}(X_\lambda)$; it then follows from (17.12.5) that this index $\lambda$ has the required property.

\begin{example}[17.12.7]
\label{IV.17.12.7}
Let $S$ be a prescheme, $G$ an $S$-\emph{group prescheme}, locally of finite presentation over $S$. Now, if $m:G\times_SG\to G$ is the morphism which defines the group prescheme structure of $G$, $m\circ(s\times1_G):S\times_SG\to G\times_SG\to G$ is an

\begin{remark}[17.12.8]
\label{IV.17.12.8}
An $S$-group prescheme $G$, of finite type (and even finite) over a locally Noetherian prescheme $S$, can be differentially smooth over $S$ without being smooth (or even \emph{flat}) over $S$. Take for example for $S$ the spectrum of the algebra of dual numbers $D=k[T]/T^2k[T]$ over a field $k$, and for $G$ the spectrum of the direct product $D$-algebra $E=D\oplus k$. We define on $G$ a structure of $S$-group scheme by defining a ``diagonal map'' $\delta$, a homomorphism from $E$ to $E\otimes_DE$: if $e'$, $e''$ are the idempotents that are the canonical images of the unit element $1$ of $D$ in the factors $D$ and $k$ of $E$, one verifies without difficulty that such a diagonal map is obtained by taking
\[
\delta(e')=e'\otimes e'+e''\otimes e'',\qquad
\delta(e'')=e'\otimes e''+e''\otimes e'.
\]
The unit section of the group scheme $G$ corresponds to the homomorphism $E\to D$ which is the identity on $D$ and $0$ on $k$; it is an isomorphism of $S$ onto a connected component of $G$, and \emph{a fortiori} $G$ is differentially smooth over $S$ (17.12.7), but it is clear that $G$ is not $S$-flat.
\end{remark}


\subsection{Transversal morphisms.}
\EGAReaderTarget{ega4.c04.subsec.17-13}{subsection:IV.17.13}
\label{IV.17.13}

\begin{env}[17.13.1]
\label{IV.17.13.1}
Let $S$ be a prescheme, $X$, $Y$, $X'$ three $S$-preschemes, $i:Y\to X$ an $S$-\emph{immersion}, $f:X'\to X$ an $S$-morphism. Set $Y'=Y\times_XX'$, and let $g:Y'\to Y$, $j:Y'\to X'$ be the canonical projections, so that we have the commutative diagram
\[
\label{IV.17.13.1.1}
\tag{17.13.1.1}
\xymatrix{
  Y \ar[d]_i & Y' \ar[l]_g \ar[d]^j\\
  X & X' \ar[l]^f
}
\]
and $j$ is an $S$-immersion. We then have a commutative diagram of quasi-coherent $\sh{O}_{Y'}$-Modules
\[
\label{IV.17.13.1.2}
\tag{17.13.1.2}
\begin{tikzcd}
  g^*(\sh{N}_{Y/X}) \ar{r} \ar{d}[swap]{\operatorname{gr}_1(g)} & f^*(\varOmega^1_{X/S})\otimes_{\sh{O}_{X'}}\sh{O}_{Y'} \ar{r} \ar{d} & g^*(\varOmega^1_{Y/S}) \ar{r} \ar{d} & 0\\
  \sh{N}_{Y'/X'} \ar{r} & \varOmega^1_{X'/S}\otimes_{\sh{O}_{X'}}\sh{O}_{Y'} \ar{r} & \varOmega^1_{Y'/S} \ar{r} & 0
\end{tikzcd}
\]
where the lower row is the exact sequence (16.4.21) applied to $j$, and the upper row comes from the same exact sequence for $i$ by application of the right exact functor $g^*$ (which therefore leaves it exact); $\operatorname{gr}_1(g)$ is defined in (16.2.1), and the commutativity of the two squares follows from (\textbf{0}, 20.5.7.3) and (\textbf{0}, 20.5.11.3).

We have moreover seen (16.2.2, (iii)) that $\operatorname{gr}_1(g)$ is surjective here, hence we deduce from (17.13.1.2) the exact sequence
\[

g^*(\sh{N}_{Y/X})\xrightarrow{\alpha}\varOmega^1_{X'/S}\otimes_{\sh{O}_{X'}}\sh{O}_{Y'}\to\varOmega^1_{Y'/S}\to0.

\end{env}

\begin{proposition}[17.13.2]
\label{IV.17.13.2}
The notation being that of \textup{(17.13.1)}, let $x'$ be a point of $Y'$, $x=g(x')$ its image in $Y$; suppose $X$ and $Y$ smooth over $S$ at the point $x$, and $X'$ smooth over $S$ at the point $x'$. Let $m$, $m-c$ be the relative dimensions of $X$ and $Y$ over $S$ at the point $x$ \textup{(17.10.1)}, and $n$ that of $X'$ over $S$ at the point $x'$. The following conditions are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}
  \item[\textup{a)}] $Y'$ is smooth over $S$ at the point $x'$ and of relative dimension $n-c$.
  \item[\textup{b)}] The homomorphism $\alpha\otimes1:g^*(\sh{N}_{Y/X})\otimes_{\sh{O}_{Y'}}\boldsymbol{k}(x')\to\varOmega^1_{X'/S}\otimes_{\sh{O}_{X'}}\boldsymbol{k}(x')$ deduced from the homomorphism $\alpha$ of \textup{(17.13.1.3)} is injective.
\end{enumerate}
Let $s$ be the canonical image of $x$ in $S$, and suppose that $x'$ is rational over $\boldsymbol{k}(s)$. Then conditions a) and b) are also equivalent to the following:
\begin{enumerate}
  \item[\textup{b$'$)}] The homomorphism transpose to $\alpha\otimes1$
  \[
  \mathrm{T}_{X'/S}(x')\to(\sh{N}_{Y/X}\otimes_{\sh{O}_Y}\boldsymbol{k}(x))^\vee=\mathrm{T}_{X/S}(x)/\mathrm{T}_{Y/S}(x)
  \]
  \textup{(cf. (16.5.12))} is surjective.
\end{enumerate}
Moreover, when conditions a) and b) are satisfied at $x'$, they are satisfied in a neighbourhood of $x'$ in $Y'$; replacing $X'$ by a neighbourhood of $x'$ if necessary, the homomorphism
\[
\operatorname{gr}_1(g):g^*(\sh{N}_{Y/X})\to\sh{N}_{Y'/X'}
\]
is bijective, and the sequence
\[
\label{IV.17.13.2.1}
\tag{17.13.2.1}
0\to g^*(\sh{N}_{Y/X})\xrightarrow{\alpha}\varOmega^1_{X'/S}\otimes_{\sh{O}_{X'}}\sh{O}_{Y'}\to\varOmega^1_{Y'/S}\to0
\]
obtained by adding a $0$ to \textup{(17.13.1.3)} is exact.
\end{proposition}

The fact that if the equivalent conditions a) and b) are satisfied at the point $x'$, they are also satisfied in a neighbourhood of $x'$ in $Y'$, follows from the fact that the set of points where a morphism is smooth is open (17.3.7) and from (17.10.2).

By virtue of (17.12.1) applied to $X'$ and $Y'$, to say that $Y'$ is smooth over $S$ at the point $x'$ is equivalent to saying that the homomorphism
\[
\delta\otimes1:\sh{N}_{Y'/X'}\otimes_{\sh{O}_{Y'}}\boldsymbol{k}(x')\to\varOmega^1_{X'/S}\otimes_{\sh{O}_{X'}}\boldsymbol{k}(x')
\]
is injective, taking into account (\textbf{0}, 19.1.12) and the fact that $\varOmega^1_{X'/S}$ is an $\sh{O}_{X'}$-Module locally free at the point $x'$ (17.2.3); since then $\varOmega^1_{Y'/S}$ is also an $\sh{O}_{Y'}$-Module locally free in a neighbourhood of $x'$ and the sequence
\[
\label{IV.17.13.2.2}
\tag{17.13.2.2}
0\to\sh{N}_{Y'/X'}\to\varOmega^1_{X'/S}\otimes_{\sh{O}_{X'}}\sh{O}_{Y'}\to\varOmega^1_{Y'/S}\to0
\]
is exact (17.2.5), to say that $Y'$ is of relative dimension $n-c$ over $S$ at the point $x'$ means, by (17.10.2), that $\sh{N}_{Y'/X'}$ (which is locally free in a neighbourhood of $x'$) is of rank $c$ at the point $x'$. But by virtue of the hypotheses on $X$ and $Y$ at the point $x$, and by the same reasoning, $\sh{N}_{Y/X}$ is locally free and of rank $c$ at the point $x$, hence $g^*(\sh{N}_{Y/X})$ is also locally free and of rank $c$ at the point $x'$. Since the homomorphism $\operatorname{gr}_1(g):g^*(\sh{N}_{Y/X})\to\sh{N}_{Y'/X'}$ is surjective, the preceding conditions are equivalent to saying that this homomorphism is
----- English, ega4/ega4-17.tex, page IV-4 91 -----
\oldpage[IV-4]{91}
bijective at the point $x'$ (Bourbaki, \emph{Alg. comm.}, chap. II, § 3, n\textsuperscript{o} 2, cor. of prop. 6), hence also in a neighbourhood of $x'$ ($\mathbf{0}_{\mathrm I}$, 5.2.7); this evidently implies b), as well as the last assertion of the statement, by virtue of the exactness of (17.13.2.2). Conversely, since $\alpha\otimes1$ factors as
\[
g^*(\sh{N}_{Y/X})\otimes\boldsymbol{k}(x')\xrightarrow{\operatorname{gr}_1(g)\otimes1}\sh{N}_{Y'/X'}\otimes\boldsymbol{k}(x')\xrightarrow{\delta\otimes1}\varOmega^1_{X'/S}\otimes\boldsymbol{k}(x')
\]
and $\operatorname{gr}_1(g)$ is surjective, to say that $\alpha\otimes1$ is injective implies that $\delta\otimes1$ is injective and that $\operatorname{gr}_1(g)\otimes1$ is bijective. We conclude from this (17.12.1) that $Y'$ is smooth over $S$ at the point $x'$, and that $\operatorname{gr}_1(g)$ is bijective in a neighbourhood of $x'$ (Bourbaki, \emph{loc. cit.}). Moreover, since the sequence (17.13.2.2) is then exact, and $\sh{N}_{Y'/X'}$, being isomorphic to $g^*(\sh{N}_{Y/X})$, is of rank $c$ at the point $x'$, $\varOmega^1_{Y'/S}$ is of rank $n-c$ at this point, which completes the proof of the equivalence of a) and b), by virtue of (17.10.2).

It remains to show the equivalence of b) and b') when $x'$ is rational over $\boldsymbol{k}(s)$ (which implies that the same holds for $x$); then $g^*(\sh{N}_{Y/X})\otimes_{\sh{O}_{Y'}}\boldsymbol{k}(x')$ is identified with $\sh{N}_{Y/X}\otimes_{\sh{O}_Y}\boldsymbol{k}(x)$, and since the sequence
\[
0\to\sh{N}_{Y/X}\to\varOmega^1_{X/S}\otimes_{\sh{O}_X}\sh{O}_Y\to\varOmega^1_{Y/S}\to0
\]
is exact at the point $x$ and formed of locally free $\sh{O}_Y$-Modules, the dual of $\sh{N}_{Y/X}\otimes_{\sh{O}_Y}\boldsymbol{k}(x)$ is identified with the quotient space $\mathrm{T}_{X/S}(x)/\mathrm{T}_{Y/S}(x)$ (16.5.12); whence the equivalence of b) and b').

\begin{definition}[17.13.3]\label{IV.17.13.3}
\EGAReaderTarget{ega4.c04.def.17-13-3}{IV.17.13.3}
With the notation of \textup{(17.13.1)}, we say that the morphism $f$ is transversal to $Y$ at the point $x'$, relative to $S$, if $X$ and $Y$ are smooth over $S$ at the point $x$, $X'$ smooth over $S$ at the point $x'$, and if the equivalent conditions a), b) of (17.13.2) are satisfied.
\end{definition}

When no confusion is to be feared, we omit mention of the prescheme $S$ and simply say that $f$ is transversal to $Y$ at the point $x'$.

\begin{remarks}[17.13.4]
\label{IV.17.13.4}
(i) Suppose that $X$, $Y$ and $X'$ are \emph{flat and locally of finite presentation} over $S$. For every $s\in S$, denote by $X_s$, $Y_s$, $X'_s$, $Y'_s$ the fibres of $X$, $Y$, $X'$, $Y'$ at the point $s$, and by $f_s:X'_s\to X_s$ the morphism deduced from $f$ by base change. It then follows from (17.5.9) that for $f$ to be a morphism transversal to $Y$ at the point $x'$, it is necessary and sufficient that, if $s$ is the image of $x$ in $S$, $f_s$ be transversal to $Y_s$ at the point $x'$, relative to $\boldsymbol{k}(s)$.

(ii) We have seen in (17.13.2) that the set of points $x'\in Y'$ where $f$ is transversal to $Y$ is \emph{open} in $Y'$; if moreover $Y'$ is \emph{proper} over $S$, we deduce that the set of $s\in S$ such that $f$ is transversal to $Y$ at all the points of $Y'_s$, relative to $S$, is \emph{open} in $S$.

When $X$, $Y$ and $X'$ are flat and locally of finite presentation over $S$, it follows from (i) that the set of $x'\in Y'$ such that $f_s$ is transversal to $Y_s$ at the point $x'$ ($s$ the image of $x'$ in $S$), relative to $\boldsymbol{k}(s)$, is \emph{open} in $Y'$. If moreover $Y'$ is proper over $S$, the set of $s\in S$ such that $f_s$ is transversal to $Y_s$ at all the points of $Y'_s$ (relative to $\boldsymbol{k}(s)$) is \emph{open} in $S$.

(iii) Since the property of being smooth over $S$, as well as the notion of relative dimension over $S$ at a point, are stable under every base change $S'\to S$ ((17.3.3) and (4.2.7)), the same holds for the property, for a morphism $f:X'\to X$, of being transversal to a sub-prescheme of $X$ at a point, relative to $S$.

\end{enumerate}

\begin{env}[17.13.5]
\label{IV.17.13.5}
Consider now a prescheme $S$, three $S$-preschemes $X$, $Y$, $Z$, and two $S$-morphisms $f:Y\to X$, $g:Z\to X$; set $T=Y\times_XZ$; we then know (\textbf{I}, 5.3.5) that we have a commutative diagram
\[
\label{IV.17.13.5.1}
\tag{17.13.5.1}
\xymatrix{
  X \ar[d]_{\Delta} & Y\times_X Z=T \ar[l] \ar[d]^{j}\\
  X\times_S X & Y\times_S Z \ar[l]^{u}
}
\]
making $T$ the product of the $(X\times_SX)$-preschemes $X$ and $Y\times_SZ$, where $u=f\times_Sg$. Since $\Delta$ is an $S$-immersion (\textbf{I}, 5.3.9), we are in the situation of the diagram (17.13.1.1); what corresponds to $\varOmega^1_{X'/S}$ in (17.13.1) is then $(\varOmega^1_{Y/S}\otimes_{\sh{O}_Y}\sh{O}_{Y\times_SZ})\oplus(\varOmega^1_{Z/S}\otimes_{\sh{O}_Z}\sh{O}_{Y\times_SZ})$ by virtue of (16.4.23). On the other hand, what corresponds to the $\sh{O}_Y$-Module $\sh{N}_{Y/X}$ in (17.13.1) is here by definition $\varOmega^1_{X/S}$ (16.3.1); to (17.13.1.3) there therefore corresponds an exact sequence
\[
\label{IV.17.13.5.2}
\tag{17.13.5.2}
\varOmega^1_{X/S}\otimes_{\sh{O}_X}\sh{O}_T\xrightarrow{\rho}
(\varOmega^1_{Y/S}\otimes_{\sh{O}_Y}\sh{O}_T)\oplus
(\varOmega^1_{Z/S}\otimes_{\sh{O}_Z}\sh{O}_T)
\xrightarrow{\sigma}\varOmega^1_{T/S}\to0
\]
where it remains to make the homomorphisms $\rho$ and $\sigma$ explicit. Taking into account first (16.4.23) and (\textbf{0}, 20.5.2), we see that if $p:T\to Y$, $q:T\to Z$ are the canonical projections, we have, with the notation of (16.4.19),
\[
\label{IV.17.13.5.3}
\tag{17.13.5.3}
\sigma=p_{T/Y/S}+q_{T/Z/S}.
\]
On the other hand, to evaluate $\rho$, let us use the commutativity of the left-hand square in (17.13.1.2), which, in the present case, comes down first to making explicit the canonical homomorphism
\[
\rho':\varOmega^1_{X/S}\to\varOmega^1_{(X\times_SX)/S}\otimes_{\sh{O}_{X\times_SX}}\sh{O}_X
\]
defined in (16.4.21) applied to the immersion $\Delta$. We may restrict to the case where $S=\Spec(A)$, $X=\Spec(B)$ are affine, and then $\rho'$ corresponds to the homomorphism $\delta$ of (\textbf{0}, 20.5.11.2), where $B$ must be replaced by $B\otimes_AB$ and $C$ by $B=(B\otimes_AB)/\mathfrak J_{B/A}$. We then see that $\delta$ maps the class of $x\otimes1-1\otimes x$ mod. $\mathfrak J_{B/A}^2$ (for an $x\in B$) to the image of
\[
(x\otimes1-1\otimes x)\otimes(1\otimes1)-(1\otimes1)\otimes(x\otimes1-1\otimes x)
\]
in $\Omega^1_{(B\otimes_AB)/A}\otimes_{(B\otimes_AB)}B$; but the preceding element can be written
\[
\begin{split}
&((x\otimes1)\otimes(1\otimes1)-(1\otimes1)\otimes(x\otimes1))\\
&\quad-((1\otimes x)\otimes(1\otimes1)-(1\otimes1)\otimes(1\otimes x))
\end{split}
\]
and we therefore see that $\rho'$ is the difference of the two homomorphisms $\pi^{(1)}$ and $\pi^{(2)}$ from $\varOmega^1_{X/S}$ to $\varOmega^1_{(X\times_SX)/S}\otimes_{\sh{O}_{X\times_SX}}\sh{O}_X$ corresponding respectively to the first and to the second projection of $X\times_SX$ to $X$, by (16.4.3.3). To obtain $\rho$, we must first consider the homomorphism
\[
\rho'':\varOmega^1_{(X\times_SX)/S}\otimes_{\sh{O}_{X\times_SX}}\sh{O}_{(Y\times_SZ)}\to\varOmega^1_{(Y\times_SZ)/S}
\]
corresponding by (16.4.3.3) to the morphism $u$ of (17.13.5.1), then, after tensoring with $\sh{O}_T$, form the composite $(\rho''\otimes1)\circ(\rho'\otimes1)$; it follows from the above that we have, with the notation of (16.4.18)
\[
\label{IV.17.13.5.4}
\tag{17.13.5.4}
\rho=(f_{Y/X/S}\otimes1_{\sh{O}_T})\oplus(-g_{Z/X/S}\otimes1_{\sh{O}_T}).
\]

\begin{definition}[17.13.3]
\EGAReaderTarget{ega4.c04.def.17-13-3}{IV.17.13.3}
Avec les notations de \textup{(17.13.1)}, on dit que le morphisme $f$ est transversal à $Y$ au point $x'$, relativement à $S$, si $X$ et $Y$ sont lisses sur $S$ au point $x$, $X'$

[id 20236]
OLD: équivalentes a), b) de (17.13.2) sont
NEW: équivalentes \textup{a), b)} de \textup{(17.13.2)} sont
FRENCH CONTEXT (corrected): .c04.def.17-13-3}{IV.17.13.3}
Avec les notations de \textup{(17.13.1)}, on dit que le morphisme $f$ est transversal à $Y$ au point $x'$, relativement à $S$, si $X$ et $Y$ sont lisses sur $S$ au point $x$, $X'$ lisse sur $S$ au point $x'$, et si les conditions équivalentes \textup{a), b)} de \textup{(17.13.2)} sont satisfaites.
\end{definition}

Lorsque aucune confusion n'est à craindre, on supprime la mention du préschéma $S$ et on dit simplement que $f$ est transversal à $Y$ au point $x'$.

This being said, the application of (17.13.2) to the situation of the diagram (17.13.5.1) (taking into account (17.3.3, (iv)), which implies that $X\times_SX$ is smooth over $S$ at $x$ if $X$ is) gives the following corollary.
\end{env}

\begin{corollary}[17.13.6]\label{IV.17.13.6}
\EGAReaderTarget{ega4.c04.cor.17-13-6}{IV.17.13.6}
Let $S$ be a prescheme, $X$, $Y$, $Z$ three $S$-preschemes $f:Y\to X$, $g:Z\to X$ two $S$-morphisms; set $T=Y\times_XZ$, and let $p:T\to Y$, $q:T\to Z$ be the canonical projections. Let $t$ be a point of $T$, $y=p(t)$, $z=q(t)$, $x=f(y)=g(z)$. Suppose that $X$ is smooth over $S$ at the point $x$, of relative dimension $m$, and $Y$ (resp. $Z$) smooth over $S$ at the point $y$ (resp. $z$), of relative dimension $m+a$ (resp. $m+b$), $a$ and $b$ being positive or negative. Then the following conditions are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}
  \item[\textup{a)}] $T$ is smooth over $S$ at the point $t$, of relative dimension $m+a+b$.
  \item[\textup{b)}] The homomorphism
  \[
  \rho\otimes1:\varOmega^1_{X/S}\otimes_{\sh{O}_X}\boldsymbol{k}(t)\to
  (\varOmega^1_{Y/S}\otimes_{\sh{O}_Y}\boldsymbol{k}(t))\oplus
  (\varOmega^1_{Z/S}\otimes_{\sh{O}_Z}\boldsymbol{k}(t))
  \]
  where $\rho$ is given by \textup{(17.13.5.4)}, is injective.
  \item[\textup{c)}] The morphism $Y\times_SZ\to X\times_SX$ is transversal to the diagonal of $X\times_SX$ at the point $t$.
\end{enumerate}
If $t$ is rational over the residue field of its image $s$ in $S$, these conditions are also equivalent to the following:
\begin{enumerate}
  \item[\textup{b$'$)}] The homomorphism
  \[
  \label{IV.17.13.6.1}
  \tag{17.13.6.1}
  \mathrm{T}_y(f)-\mathrm{T}_z(g):\mathrm{T}_{Y/S}(y)\oplus\mathrm{T}_{Z/S}(z)\to\mathrm{T}_{X/S}(x)
  \]
  \textup{(cf. (16.5.12.5))} is surjective.
\end{enumerate}
Moreover, when conditions a) and b) are satisfied at $t$, they are satisfied in a neighbourhood of $t$ in $T$, and, on restricting $T$ to such a neighbourhood, the sequence
\[
\label{IV.17.13.6.2}
\tag{17.13.6.2}
0\to\varOmega^1_{X/S}\otimes_{\sh{O}_X}\sh{O}_T\xrightarrow{\rho}
(\varOmega^1_{Y/S}\otimes_{\sh{O}_Y}\sh{O}_T)\oplus
(\varOmega^1_{Z/S}\otimes_{\sh{O}_Z}\sh{O}_T)
\xrightarrow{\sigma}\varOmega^1_{T/S}\to0
\]
(where $\sigma$ is given by (17.13.5.3)) is exact.
\end{corollary}

The only point that remains to be proved in (17.13.6) concerns \emph{b')}, and follows from the fact that, under the hypothesis that $t$ is rational over $\boldsymbol{k}(s)$, the homomorphism $\rho\otimes1$ is the \emph{transpose} of $\mathrm{T}_y(f)-\mathrm{T}_z(g)$, by virtue of (17.13.5.4).

When the equivalent conditions of (17.13.6) are satisfied, we say that $f$ and $g$ form a \emph{pair of morphisms transversal at the point $t$, relative} to $S$; here again, mention of $S$ is often omitted.

Consider in particular the case where $Y$ and $Z$ are \emph{sub-preschemes} of $X$, $f$ and $g$ being the canonical injections; $T$ is then the ``intersection'' sub-prescheme

Denoting by $X_s$, $Y_s$, $Z_s$, $T_s$ the fibres of $X$, $Y$, $Z$, $T$ at the point $s$, the image of $x$ in $S$, and taking into account (5.2.3), (5.1.9) and (\textbf{0}, 16.5.12), we see that for this to be the case (when $X$, $Y$, $Z$ are smooth over $S$ at the point $x$), it is necessary and sufficient that $T$ be smooth over $S$ at the point $x$ and that we have the relation

\begin{proposition}[17.13.8]
\label{IV.17.13.8}
Let $S$ be a prescheme, $X$ an $S$-prescheme locally of finite presentation over $S$, $Y$, $Z$ two sub-preschemes of $X$, $T=\inf(Y,Z)$ the ``intersection'' sub-prescheme of $Y$ and $Z$, and $x$ a point of $T$. The following conditions are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}
  \item[\textup{a)}] The canonical injection $i:Y\to X$ is a morphism transversal to $Z$ at the point $x$, relative to $S$ \textup{(17.13.3)}.
  \item[\textup{a$'$)}] The canonical injection $j:Z\to X$ is a morphism transversal to $Y$ at the point $x$, relative to $S$.
  \item[\textup{a$''$)}] $Y$ and $Z$ intersect transversally at the point $x$, relative to $S$.
  \item[\textup{b)}] $X$, $Y$, $Z$ are smooth over $S$ at the point $x$, and the homomorphism $\rho$ \textup{(17.13.5.4)} is such that
  \[
  \rho\otimes1:\varOmega^1_{X/S}\otimes_{\sh{O}_X}\boldsymbol{k}(x)\to
  (\varOmega^1_{Y/S}\otimes_{\sh{O}_Y}\boldsymbol{k}(x))\oplus
  (\varOmega^1_{Z/S}\otimes_{\sh{O}_Z}\boldsymbol{k}(x))
  \]
  is injective.
\end{enumerate}
When $x$ is rational over $\boldsymbol{k}(s)$ ($s$ the image of $x$ in $S$), these conditions are also equivalent to the following:
\begin{enumerate}
  \item[\textup{b$'$)}] The homomorphism
  \[
  \mathrm{T}_x(i)-\mathrm{T}_x(j):\mathrm{T}_{Y/S}(x)\oplus\mathrm{T}_{Z/S}(x)\to\mathrm{T}_{X/S}(x)
  \]
  is surjective.
\end{enumerate}
Moreover, when the equivalent conditions a) to b) are satisfied at the point $x$, they are satisfied in a neighbourhood of $x$ in $T$, and, on restricting $X$ to a neighbourhood of $x$, the sequence (17.13.6.2) is exact, and we have a canonical isomorphism
\[
\label{IV.17.13.8.1}
\tag{17.13.8.1}
\sh{N}_{T/X}\xrightarrow{\sim}
(\sh{N}_{Y/X}\otimes_{\sh{O}_Y}\sh{O}_T)\oplus
(\sh{N}_{Z/X}\otimes_{\sh{O}_Z}\sh{O}_T).
\]
\end{proposition}

Conditions \emph{a)}, \emph{a')}, \emph{a'')} all imply that $X$, $Y$, $Z$ are smooth over $S$ at the point $x$. Moreover, let $m$, $m-a$, $m-b$ be the relative dimensions of $X$, $Y$, $Z$ over $S$ at the point $x$. It then follows from (17.13.2), applied with $X'$ replaced by $Z$ and $Y'$ by $T=Y\times_XZ$, that conditions \emph{a)}, \emph{a')} are both equivalent to saying that $T$ is smooth over $S$ at the point $x$ and of relative dimension $m-a-b$ at this point; but by virtue of (17.13.7), this means precisely that condition a'') is satisfied, whence the equivalence of a), a') and a''). The equivalence of a'') and b) (or b') when $x$ is rational over $\boldsymbol{k}(s)$) was proved in (17.13.6), as was the fact that if these conditions are satisfied at the point $x$, they are satisfied in a neighbourhood of $x$, and the exactness of the sequence (17.13.6.2) in such a neighbourhood. It remains to define the canonical isomorphism (17.13.8.1).

  \[

  \tag{17.13.6.1}
  \mathrm{T}_y(f)-\mathrm{T}_z(g):\mathrm{T}_{Y/S}(y)\oplus\mathrm{T}_{Z/S}(z)\to\mathrm{T}_{X/S}(x)
  \]
  \textup{(cf. (16.5.12.5))} est surjectif.
\end{enumerate}

Denote by $\alpha$ and $-\beta$ the homomorphisms appearing on the right-hand side of (17.13.5.4), and by $\gamma$ and $\delta$ those appearing on the right-hand side of (17.13.5.2). To say that the sequence (17.13.6.2)
\[
0\to\varOmega^1_{X/S}\otimes_{\sh{O}_X}\sh{O}_T
\xrightarrow{\alpha\oplus(-\beta)}
(\varOmega^1_{Y/S}\otimes_{\sh{O}_Y}\sh{O}_T)\oplus
(\varOmega^1_{Z/S}\otimes_{\sh{O}_Z}\sh{O}_T)
\xrightarrow{\gamma+\delta}\varOmega^1_{T/S}\to0
\]
is exact means that, in the category of $\sh{O}_T$-Modules, $\varOmega^1_{X/S}\otimes_{\sh{O}_X}\sh{O}_T$ is canonically identified with the \emph{fibre product} of $\varOmega^1_{Y/S}\otimes_{\sh{O}_Y}\sh{O}_T$ and $\varOmega^1_{Z/S}\otimes_{\sh{O}_Z}\sh{O}_T$ over $\varOmega^1_{T/S}$, for the homomorphisms $\gamma$ and $\delta$. The same reasoning as in (\textbf{0}, 18.1.2 and 18.1.3), where rings and two-sided ideals are replaced respectively by modules and submodules, yields a commutative diagram
{\scriptsize
\[
\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=small,row sep=large]
0 \ar[dr] & & 0 \ar[d] & 0 \ar[d] & \\
& (\sh{N}_{Y/X}\otimes\sh{O}_T)\oplus(\sh{N}_{Z/X}\otimes\sh{O}_T)
  \ar[r] \ar[d] \ar[dr,"\iota"]
& \sh{N}_{Z/X}\otimes\sh{O}_T \ar[r,"\sim"] \ar[d]
& \sh{N}_{T/Y} \ar[d] & \\
0 \ar[r] & \sh{N}_{Y/X}\otimes\sh{O}_T \ar[r] \ar[d,"\wr"]
& \varOmega^1_{X/S}\otimes\sh{O}_T \ar[r,"\alpha"] \ar[d,"\beta" swap] \ar[dr,"\varepsilon"]
& \varOmega^1_{Y/S}\otimes\sh{O}_T \ar[r] \ar[d,"\gamma"] & 0\\
0 \ar[r] & \sh{N}_{T/Z} \ar[r]
& \varOmega^1_{Z/S}\otimes\sh{O}_T \ar[r,"\delta" swap] \ar[d]
& \varOmega^1_{T/S} \ar[r] \ar[d] \ar[dr] & 0\\
& & 0 & 0 & 0
\end{tikzcd}
\]
}
where the 3\textsuperscript{rd} and 4\textsuperscript{th} rows and columns are exact by virtue of the smoothness hypotheses. The fact that the composite homomorphism $\varepsilon=\gamma\circ\alpha=\delta\circ\beta$ is the homomorphism corresponding to the canonical injection $T\to X$ follows from (\textbf{0}, 20.5.2.7). Since the diagonal of the preceding diagram is exact, $\operatorname{Ker}(\varepsilon)$ is canonically identified with $\sh{N}_{T/X}$, taking into account the fact that $T$ is smooth over $S$ (17.2.5); hence we have a canonical isomorphism (17.13.8.1), inverse to $\iota$.

\begin{env}[17.13.9]
\label{IV.17.13.9}
We can generalize the results of (17.13.5) and (17.13.6) to an arbitrary finite number of $S$-morphisms $f_i:Y_i\to X$ ($i\in I$, $I$ a finite set). For this, denote by $X^I$ the product of the family $(X_i)_{i\in I}$ of $S$-preschemes all identical to $X$ (\textbf{I}, 3.3.5), and let $p_i$ ($i\in I$) be the canonical projections. The \emph{diagonal morphism} $\Delta:X\to X^I$ is defined (as in (\textbf{I}, 5.3.1)) as the unique $S$-morphism such that $p_i\circ\Delta=1_X$ for every $i\in I$. It is an $X$-section of $X^I$ for the morphism $p_1$, hence (\textbf{I}, 5.3.11) an \emph{immersion}.

Let then $Y$ be the product of the $S$-preschemes $Y_i$ (for the composite morphisms $Y_i\xrightarrow{f_i}X\to S$), and $T$ the product of the $X$-preschemes $Y_i$ (for the morphisms $f_i$). We prove as in (\textbf{I}, 5.3.5) that we have a commutative diagram
\[
\label{IV.17.13.9.1}
\tag{17.13.9.1}
\xymatrix{
  X \ar[d]_{\Delta} & T \ar[l]_-v \ar[d]^{j}\\
  X^I & Y \ar[l]^{u}
}
\]
(where $u$ is the product (over $S$) of the $f_i$), which makes $T$ the product of the $X^I$-preschemes $X$ and $Y$.

By induction on the number of elements of $I$, it follows from (16.4.23) that $\varOmega^1_{X^I/S}$ (resp. $\varOmega^1_{Y/S}$) is canonically identified with the direct sum of the $p_i^*(\varOmega^1_{X/S})$ (resp. of the $q_i^*(\varOmega^1_{Y_i/S})$, if $q_i:Y\to Y_i$ are the canonical projections).

Suppose now that $X$ is \emph{smooth} over $S$ at a point $x$ (hence in a neighbourhood of this point). The same then holds for $X^I$ at this point (17.3.3, (iv)), and, restricting $X$ to a neighbourhood of $x$, we therefore have (17.2.5) an exact sequence of locally free $\sh{O}_X$-Modules
\[
0\to\mathscr{G}r_1(\Delta)\to(\varOmega^1_{X/S})^I\to\varOmega^1_{X/S}\to0.
\]
Set $\sh{J}=\mathscr{G}r_1(\Delta)$; from the preceding sequence we deduce an exact sequence of locally free $\sh{O}_T$-Modules
\[
\label{IV.17.13.9.1-seq}
\tag{17.13.9.1}
0\to\sh{J}\otimes_{\sh{O}_X}\sh{O}_T\to(\varOmega^1_{X/S})^I\otimes_{\sh{O}_X}\sh{O}_T\xrightarrow{\sigma}\varOmega^1_{X/S}\otimes_{\sh{O}_X}\sh{O}_T\to0
\]
which corresponds to the first row of the diagram (17.13.1.2), and where $\sigma$ is none other than the canonical homomorphism which, to each family of sections $(t_i)_{i\in I}$ of $\varOmega^1_{X/S}\otimes_{\sh{O}_X}\sh{O}_T$ over an open subset of $T$, assigns its \emph{sum}.

On the other hand, what corresponds here to the second vertical arrow of the diagram (17.13.1.2) is the homomorphism
\[
\label{IV.17.13.9.2}
\tag{17.13.9.2}
\tau:(\varOmega^1_{X/S})^I\otimes_{\sh{O}_X}\sh{O}_T\to\bigoplus_{i\in I}(\varOmega^1_{Y_i/S}\otimes_{\sh{O}_{Y_i}}\sh{O}_T)
\]
which, to every family $(t'_i\otimes1)_{i\in I}$, where here the $t'_i$ are sections of $\varOmega^1_{X/S}$ over an open subset of $X$, associates the sum of the $((f_i)_{Y_i/X/S}(t'_i))\otimes1$, with the notation of (16.4.18). The homomorphism $\rho$ which corresponds to the homomorphism $\alpha$ of (17.13.1.3) is therefore the \emph{restriction} to $\sh{J}\otimes_{\sh{O}_X}\sh{O}_T$ of the preceding homomorphism $\tau$.

These hypotheses and notation having been specified, we can apply prop. (17.13.2) to the situation of (17.13.9.1), which gives the
\end{env}

\begin{corollary}[17.13.10]\label{IV.17.13.10}
\EGAReaderTarget{ega4.c04.cor.17-13-10}{IV.17.13.10}
Under the general hypotheses of \textup{(17.13.9)}, let $t$ be a point of $T$, $y_i$ ($i\in I$) its projections in the $Y_i$, and $x$ the point of $X$ equal to each of the $f_i(y_i)$. Suppose that $X$ is smooth over $S$ at the point $x$ and of relative dimension $d$, and that each of the $Y_i$ is smooth over $S$ at the point $y_i$; let $d+c_i$ ($c_i$ a positive or negative integer) be the relative dimension of $Y_i$ at the point $y_i$. Then the following conditions are equivalent:
----- English, ega4/ega4-17.tex, page IV-4 97 -----
\oldpage[IV-4]{97}

\begin{enumerate}
  \item[\textup{a)}] $T$ is smooth over $S$ at the point $t$, of relative dimension $d+\sum_{i\in I}c_i$.
  \item[\textup{b)}] The homomorphism
  \[
  \rho\otimes1:\sh{J}\otimes_{\sh{O}_X}\boldsymbol{k}(t)\to\bigoplus_{i\in I}(\varOmega^1_{Y_i/S}\otimes_{\sh{O}_{Y_i}}\boldsymbol{k}(t))
  \]
  is injective.
\end{enumerate}
When $t$ is rational over the field $\boldsymbol{k}(s)$ (where $s$ is the image of $t$ in $S$), these conditions are also equivalent to the following:
\begin{enumerate}
  \item[\textup{b$'$)}] The homomorphism transpose to $\rho\otimes1$
  \[
  \bigoplus_{i\in I}\mathrm{T}_{Y_i/X}(y_i)\to(\mathrm{T}_{X/S}(x))^I/\delta(\mathrm{T}_{X/S}(x))
  \]
  (where $\delta$ is the diagonal map) is surjective.
\end{enumerate}
\end{corollary}

It suffices to remark that $X^I$ is smooth over $S$ and of relative dimension $d.\operatorname{Card}(I)$, and $Y$ smooth over $S$ of relative dimension $d.\operatorname{Card}(I)+\sum_{i\in I}c_i$.

When the equivalent conditions of (17.13.10) are satisfied, we again say that the $f_i$ form a \emph{family of morphisms transversal at the point $t$, relative} to $S$. We again see that the set of points $t\in T$ where this holds is \emph{open} in $T$. Remark (17.13.4, (ii)) then shows that if $X$ and the $Y_i$ are flat and locally of finite presentation over $S$, and if moreover $T$ is \emph{proper} over $S$, the set of $s\in S$ such that the $f_i$ form a family of transversal morphisms at \emph{all} the points of $T_s$, relative to $S$, is \emph{open} in $S$.

\begin{env}[17.13.11]\label{IV.17.13.11}
\EGAReaderTarget{ega4.c04.statement.17-13-11}{IV.17.13.11}
Consider in particular the case, generalizing (17.13.7), where the $Y_i$ ($i\in I$) are \emph{sub-preschemes} of $X$, the $f_i$ being the canonical injections, so that $T=\inf_{i\in I}(Y_i)$ is again the ``intersection'' sub-prescheme of the $Y_i$, and $t=y_i=x$ for every $i$; instead of saying that the $f_i$ form a family of transversal morphisms at the point $x$, we again say that \emph{the $Y_i$ intersect transversally at the point $x$} (relative to $S$). Condition a) of (17.13.10) is again expressed by the relation, generalizing (17.13.7.1),
\[
\label{IV.17.13.11.1}
\tag{17.13.11.1}
\operatorname{codim}_x(T_s,X_s)=\sum_{i\in I}\operatorname{codim}_x((Y_i)_s,X_s).
\]
Moreover, we have the following property, which extends (17.13.8.1) and gives another proof of it when $I$ has 2 elements:
\end{env}

\begin{corollary}[17.13.12]
\label{IV.17.13.12}
When the $Y_i$ intersect transversally at the point $x$, we have a canonical isomorphism
\[
\label{IV.17.13.12.1}
\tag{17.13.12.1}
\sh{N}_{T/X}\xrightarrow{\sim}\bigoplus_{i\in I}(\sh{N}_{Y_i/X}\otimes_{\sh{O}_{Y_i}}\sh{O}_T).
\]
\end{corollary}

We may restrict to the case where the $Y_i$ are closed sub-preschemes of $X$, defined by quasi-coherent ideals $\sh{J}_i$, so that $T$ is defined by the ideal $\sh{J}=\sum_i\sh{J}_i$. By definition of the conormal sheaf of an immersion (16.1.3), the canonical homomorphism $\bigoplus_i\sh{J}_i\to\sh{J}$ gives, by passage to quotients, a \emph{surjective} homomorphism
\[
\label{IV.17.13.12.2}
\tag{17.13.12.2}
\bigoplus_{i\in I}(\sh{N}_{Y_i/X}\otimes_{\sh{O}_{Y_i}}\sh{O}_T)\to\sh{N}_{T/X}.
\]

\subsection{Local and infinitesimal characterizations of smooth morphisms, unramified morphisms and étale morphisms.}
\EGAReaderTarget{ega4.c04.subsec.17-14}{subsection:IV.17.14}

\begin{proposition}[17.14.1]
\label{IV.17.14.1}
Let $f:X\to Y$ be a morphism locally of finite presentation, $x$ a point of $X$, $y=f(x)$. For $f$ to be smooth (resp. unramified, resp. étale) at the point $x$, it is necessary and sufficient that the following condition be satisfied:

For every local scheme $Y'=\operatorname{Spec}(A')$ with closed point $y'$, every morphism $h:Y'\to Y$ such that $h(y')=y$, every closed subscheme $Y'_0=\operatorname{Spec}(A'/\mathfrak{J}')$ of $Y'$, where the ideal $\mathfrak{J}'$ is of square zero, and every $Y$-morphism $g_0:Y'_0\to X$ such that $g_0(y')=x$, there exists at least one (resp. at most one, resp. one and only one) $Y$-morphism $g:Y'\to X$ whose restriction to $Y'_0$ is $g_0$.
\end{proposition}

Taking into account the definitions (17.1.1 and 17.3.1), we have to show that the condition of the statement is \emph{sufficient} for $f$ to be smooth (resp. unramified) at the point $x$.

(i) \emph{Case of smooth morphisms.} We may restrict to the case where $Y=\operatorname{Spec}(A)$ and $X=\operatorname{Spec}(C)$ are affine, with $C=B/\mathfrak{K}$, where $B=A[T_1,\ldots,T_n]$ is a polynomial $A$-algebra and $\mathfrak{K}$ an ideal of finite type of $B$. To prove that $f$ is smooth at the point $x$, it suffices to establish that $\sh{O}_{X,x}$ is a formally smooth $\sh{O}_{Y,y}$-algebra for the discrete topologies (17.5.1). Now we have $\sh{O}_{X,x}=B_{\mathfrak q}/\mathfrak{K}B_{\mathfrak q}$, where $\mathfrak q$ is a prime ideal of $B$, and if $\mathfrak p$ is the inverse image of $\mathfrak q$ in $A$, $B_{\mathfrak q}$ is a formally smooth $A_{\mathfrak p}$-algebra for the discrete topologies (\textbf{0}, 19.3.2 and 19.3.5). By application of the Jacobian criterion (\textbf{0}, 22.6.1 and 20.5.12) it therefore suffices to see that $B_{\mathfrak q}/\mathfrak{K}^2B_{\mathfrak q}$ is an $A_{\mathfrak p}$-trivial extension of $B_{\mathfrak q}/\mathfrak{K}B_{\mathfrak q}$ by $\mathfrak{K}B_{\mathfrak q}/\mathfrak{K}^2B_{\mathfrak q}$. But this follows precisely from the hypothesis applied to $A'=B_{\mathfrak q}/\mathfrak{K}^2B_{\mathfrak q}$ and $\mathfrak{J}'=\mathfrak{K}B_{\mathfrak q}/\mathfrak{K}^2B_{\mathfrak q}$ and to the morphism $g_0$ corresponding to the identity automorphism of $A'/\mathfrak{J}'=\sh{O}_{X,x}$.

(ii) \emph{Case of unramified morphisms.} Here set $A=\sh{O}_{Y,y}$, $B=\sh{O}_{X,x}$, and note that by virtue of (17.4.1, \emph{c)}), it suffices to show that $\Omega^1_{B/A}=(\varOmega^1_{X/Y})_x=0$. Now, with the notation of (\textbf{0}, 20.4.1), the ring $C=\mathrm{P}^1_{B/A}=(B\otimes_A B)/\mathfrak{J}^2$ is local, since the same holds for $C/(\mathfrak{J}/\mathfrak{J}^2)$, which is isomorphic to $B$. The hypothesis applied to $A'=C$ and $\mathfrak{J}'=\mathfrak{J}/\mathfrak{J}^2$ shows by definition that the map $d_{B/A}:B\to\Omega^1_{B/A}$ is zero (\textbf{0}, 20.4.6), hence that $\Omega^1_{B/A}=0$ by (\textbf{0}, 20.4.7).

\begin{proposition}[17.14.2]
\label{IV.17.14.2}
Let $Y$ be a locally Noetherian prescheme, $f:X\to Y$ a morphism locally of finite type, $x$ a point of $X$, $y=f(x)$. For $f$ to be smooth (resp. unramified, resp. étale) at the point $x$, it is necessary and sufficient that it satisfy the condition of \textup{(17.14.1)} where we suppose moreover that the local ring $A'$ is Artinian, that $\mathfrak m'\mathfrak{J}'=0$, where $\mathfrak m'$ is the maximal ideal of $A'$, and that the residue field $A'/\mathfrak m'$ of $A'$ is equal to $\boldsymbol{k}(x)$.
\end{proposition}

We again have to show that these conditions are sufficient in the case of smooth morphisms and in the case of unramified morphisms.
----- French corrections on page IV-4 97 -----
[id 20362]
OLD: (où $s$ est l'image de $t$ dans $S$)
NEW: \textup{(}où $s$ est l'image de $t$ dans $S$\textup{)}

  \[
  \rho\otimes1:\sh{J}\otimes_{\sh{O}_X}\boldsymbol{k}(t)\to\bigoplus_{i\in I}(\varOmega^1_{Y_i/S}\otimes_{\sh{O}_{Y_i}}\boldsymbol{k}(t))
  \]
  est injectif.
\end{enumerate}

Lorsque $t$ est rationnel sur le corps $\boldsymbol{k}(s)$ \textup{(}où $s$ est l'image de $t$ dans $S$\textup{)}, ces conditions sont aussi équivalentes à la suivante:
\begin{enumerate}
  \item[\textup{b$'$)}] L'homomorphisme transposé de $\rho\otimes1$
  \[
  \bigoplus_{i\in I}\mathrm{T}_{Y_i/X}(y_i)\to(\mathrm{T}_{X/S}(x))^I/\delta(\mathrm{T}_{X/S}(x))

  (où $\de

Lorsque les conditions équivalentes de (17.13.10) sont vérifiées, on dit encore que les $f_i$ forment une \emph{famille de morphismes transversaux au point $t$, relativement à} $S$. On voit encore que l'ensemble des points $t\in T$ où cela a lieu est \emph{ouvert} dans $T$. La remarque (17.13.4, (ii)) montre alors que si $X$ et les $Y_i$ sont plats et localement de présentation finie sur $S$, et de plus si $T$ est \emph{propre} sur $S$, l'ensemb

----- (English page IV-4 97: shown above in this file) -----
----- (English page IV-4 98: shown above in this file) -----

(i) \emph{Case of smooth morphisms.} If we set $A=\sh{O}_{Y,y}$ and $B=\sh{O}_{X,x}$, we have to prove here, taking into account (17.5.3), that $B$ is a formally smooth $A$-algebra for the preadic topologies. Now, by virtue of (\textbf{0}, 22.1.4), this follows from the hypothesis when the condition $\mathfrak m'\mathfrak{J}'=0$ in it is replaced by $\mathfrak{J}'\subset\mathfrak m'$. But since $\mathfrak m'$ is nilpotent, we see that the condition of the statement is already sufficient by considering the rings $A'/\mathfrak m'^{j}\mathfrak{J}'$ and reasoning by induction on $j$ as in (\textbf{0}, 19.4.3).

(ii) \emph{Case of unramified morphisms.} Let us take up again the notation of the proof of (17.14.1, (ii)); to prove that the ideal $\mathfrak{J}/\mathfrak{J}^2$ of $C$ is zero, note that $C$ is then a Noetherian local ring, being a local ring of the ring of an affine open subset of a sub-prescheme of $X\times_YX$, which is locally of finite type over $X$, hence also over $Y$. If $\mathfrak r$ is the maximal ideal of $C$, it therefore suffices to verify that we have $\mathfrak{J}/\mathfrak{J}^2\subset\mathfrak r^n$ for every $n$ ($\mathbf{0}_{\mathrm I}$, 7.3.5), or equivalently that $\mathfrak r^n=\mathfrak r^n+(\mathfrak{J}/\mathfrak{J}^2)$. With the notation of (\textbf{0}, 20.4.6), this again means that for every $n$, the two composite maps $B\xrightarrow{p_1}C\to C/\mathfrak r^n$ and $B\xrightarrow{p_2}C\to C/\mathfrak r^n$ are identical; but since $C/\mathfrak r^n$ is Artinian, this identity follows from the hypothesis applied, by induction on $n$, to $A'=C/\mathfrak r^n$ and $\mathfrak{J}'=(\mathfrak r^n+(\mathfrak{J}/\mathfrak{J}^2))/\mathfrak r^n$.

\begin{remark}[17.14.3]
\label{IV.17.14.3}
Note that, under the hypotheses of (17.14.2), if moreover the field $\boldsymbol{k}(x)$ is a \emph{finite} extension of $\boldsymbol{k}(y)$, then the $A'$-modules $\mathfrak m'^{j}/\mathfrak m'^{j+1}$ are $\boldsymbol{k}(y)$-vector spaces of finite rank, hence \emph{a fortiori} $A'$ is a finite $\sh{O}_{Y,y}$-algebra.
\end{remark}


\subsection{Case of preschemes over a base field.}
\EGAReaderTarget{ega4.c04.subsec.17-15}{subsection:IV.17.15}

Let us first recall (6.7.7, 6.7.8 and 6.8.1) the following

\begin{proposition}[17.15.1]
\label{IV.17.15.1}
Let $k$ be a field and $X$ a prescheme locally of finite type over $k$. For $X$ to be smooth at a point $x$, it is necessary and sufficient that for every radicial extension $k'$ of $k$ (or only for every finite radicial extension of $k$), the local ring $\sh{O}_{X,x}\otimes_k k'$ be regular. If $\boldsymbol{k}(x)$ is a separable extension of $k$ (in particular if $k$ is perfect), it amounts to the same to say that $\sh{O}_{X,x}$ is regular.
\end{proposition}

\begin{corollary}[17.15.2]
\label{IV.17.15.2}
Under the conditions of \textup{(17.15.1)}, for $X$ to be smooth over $k$, it is necessary and sufficient that $X$ be geometrically regular over $k$ \textup{(6.7.6)}, which implies that $X$ is regular. If $k$ is perfect, then $X$ is smooth over $k$ if and only if $X$ is regular.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proposition}[17.15.3]
\label{IV.17.15.3}
Let $k$ be a field, $X$ a prescheme locally of finite type over $k$, $x$ a point of $X$, and $n=\dim_x(X)$. Let $(g_i)_{1\leq i\leq n}$ be a family of $n$ sections of $\sh{O}_X$ over $X$, and let $f:X\to Y=\operatorname{Spec}(k[T_1,\ldots,T_n])$ be the morphism corresponding to the $k$-homomorphism $k[T_1,\ldots,T_n]\to\Gamma(X,\sh{O}_X)$ that sends $T_i$ to $g_i$ \textup{(\textbf{I}, 2.2.4)}. The following conditions are equivalent (and imply that $X$ is smooth over $k$ at the point $x$, and consequently regular at the point $x$):
\begin{enumerate}
  \item[\textup{a)}] $f$ is étale at the point $x$.
  \item[\textup{b)}] The images of the $d_{X/k}g_i$ form a basis of the $\sh{O}_{X,x}$-module $(\varOmega^1_{X/k})_x$.
  \item[\textup{c)}] The images of the $d_{X/k}g_i$ generate the $\sh{O}_{X,x}$-module $(\varOmega^1_{X/k})_x$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

If $f$ is étale at the point $x$, then $X$ is smooth over $k$ at the point $x$, since $Y=\operatorname{Spec}(k[T_1,\ldots,T_n])$ is smooth over $k$ (17.3.8); the fact that a) implies b) is therefore a particular case of (17.11.3). Since b) trivially implies c), it remains to see that c) implies a). Let us first note

(i) \emph{Cas des morphismes lisses.} Si l'on pose $A=\sh{O}_

that the hypothesis implies that the homomorphism $(f^*(\varOmega^1_{Y/k}))_x\to(\varOmega^1_{X/k})_x$ is surjective; hence, replacing $X$ by an open neighbourhood of $x$, we may suppose that the homomorphism $f^*(\varOmega^1_{Y/k})\to\varOmega^1_{X/k}$ is surjective (Bourbaki, \emph{Alg. comm.}, chap. II, § 5, n\textsuperscript{o} 1, prop. 2); consequently $f$ is \emph{unramified} (17.2.2). We will first see that we may restrict to the case where $x$ is \emph{rational over $k$}. Indeed, if we set $k'=\boldsymbol{k}(x)$, and $X'=X\otimes_k k'$, $Y'=Y\otimes_k k'=\operatorname{Spec}(k'[T_1,\ldots,T_n])$, there exists a point $x'\in X'$ above $x$ such that $\boldsymbol{k}(x')=k'$. To prove that $f$ is étale at the point $x$, it suffices to show that $f'=f_{(k')}:X'\to Y'$ is étale at the point $x'$ (17.7.1, (ii)); moreover, $f'$ is unramified (17.3.3, (iii)) and we have $\dim_{x'}(X')=n$ (4.2.7). In the same way, replacing $k'$ by an algebraically closed extension of $k'$, we may suppose that $k$ is \emph{algebraically closed}. Then set $y=f(x)$, $A=\sh{O}_{Y,y}$, $B=\sh{O}_{X,x}$; since the residue field of $B$ is equal to $k$, the same holds for that of $A$, so $x$ (resp. $y$) is a closed point of $X$ (resp. $Y$) (\textbf{I}, 6.4.2), and consequently we have $\dim(A)=\dim(B)=n$. Since $f$ is unramified, the homomorphism $\widehat A\to\widehat B$ is surjective (17.4.4, f'')); but since $\dim(\widehat A)=\dim(\widehat B)=n$, and $\widehat A$, being a regular ring, is integral, the homomorphism $\widehat A\to\widehat B$ is also injective (\textbf{0}, 16.3.10). This homomorphism is therefore bijective, which completes the proof that $f$ is étale at the point $x$ (17.6.3, \emph{e'')}).

\begin{corollary}[17.15.4]
\label{IV.17.15.4}
Under the general hypotheses of (17.15.3), suppose moreover that $\boldsymbol{k}(x)$ is a finite and separable extension of $k$ and that the germs $(g_i)_x$ belong to $\mathfrak m_x$. Then the conditions a), b), c) of (17.15.3) are also equivalent to each of the following:
\begin{enumerate}
  \item[\textup{d)}] The $n$ germs $(g_i)_x$ generate the maximal ideal $\mathfrak m_x$ of $\sh{O}_{X,x}$.
  \item[\textup{d$'$)}] The ring $\sh{O}_{X,x}$ is regular and the $(g_i)_x$ form a regular system of parameters for this ring \textup{(\textbf{0}, 17.1.6)}.
\end{enumerate}
\end{corollary}

Indeed, d') trivially implies d). On the other hand, since $\boldsymbol{k}(x)$ is a finite and separable extension of $k$, we have $\Omega^1_{\kres(x)/k}=0$ (\textbf{0}, 20.6.20), and $\boldsymbol{k}(x)=\sh{O}_{X,x}/\mathfrak m_x$ is a formally smooth $k$-algebra for the discrete topologies (\textbf{0}, 19.6.1); hence the exact sequence (\textbf{0}, 20.5.14.1) applies to $A=k$, $B=\sh{O}_{X,x}$, $\mathfrak{K}=\mathfrak m_x$, and yields a canonical isomorphism
\[
\delta:\mathfrak m_x/\mathfrak m_x^2\xrightarrow{\sim}(\varOmega^1_{X/k})_x\otimes_{\sh{O}_{X,x}}\boldsymbol{k}(x).
\]
From this we first deduce the equivalence of conditions c) and d), taking into account Nakayama's lemma. On the other hand, if $f$ is étale at the point $x$, the ring $\sh{O}_{X,x}$ is regular and of dimension $n$, since $x$ is a closed point of $X$ (\textbf{I}, 6.4.2), and $n$ elements of $\mathfrak m_x$ that generate this maximal ideal then necessarily form a regular system of parameters for $\sh{O}_{X,x}$ (\textbf{0}, 17.1.6), which proves that a) implies d').

\begin{proposition}[17.15.5]
\label{IV.17.15.5}
Let $k$ be a field, $X$ a prescheme locally of finite type over $k$, $x$ a point of $X$, and $n=\dim_x(X)$. The following conditions are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}
  \item[\textup{a)}] $X$ is smooth over $k$ at the point $x$.
  \item[\textup{b)}] $X$ is differentially smooth over $k$ at the point $x$.
  \item[\textup{c)}] $(\varOmega^1_{X/k})_x$ is a free $\sh{O}_{X,x}$-module of rank $n$.
  \item[\textup{d)}] $(\varOmega^1_{X/k})_x$ is an $\sh{O}_{X,x}$-module admitting a system of $n$ generators.

Comme \emph{b)} entraîne trivialement \emph{c)}, il reste à voir que \emph{c)} implique \emph{a)}. Notons d'abord
\oldpage[IV-4]{100}
que l'hypothèse entraîne que l'homomorphisme $(f^*(\varOmega^1_{Y/k}))_x\to(\varOmega^1_{X/k})_x$ est surjectif, donc, en remplaçant $X$ par un voisinage ouvert de $x$, on peut supposer que l'homomorphisme $f^*(\varOmega^1_{

=================== PAGE IV-4 100 ===================
----- (English page IV-4 99: shown above in this file) -----
----- (English page IV-4 100: shown above in this file) -----
----- English, ega4/ega4-17.tex, page IV-4 101 -----
\oldpage[IV-4]{101}

  \item[\textup{e)}] There exist a perfect extension $k'$ of $k$ and an open neighbourhood $U$ of $x$ in $X$ such that the prescheme $U\otimes_k k'$ is regular.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

The fact that $X$ is smooth over $k$ at the point $x$ implies the existence of an open neighbourhood $U$ of $x$ that is smooth over $k$; hence $U\otimes_k k'$ is regular for every extension $k'$ of $k$ (17.15.2), which proves that a) implies e). Conversely, e) implies a), since then $U\otimes_k k'$ is smooth over $k'$ (17.15.2), hence $U$ is smooth over $k$ (17.7.1, (ii)).

We have already proved that a) implies c) (17.10.2); c) implies d) trivially, and the fact that d) implies a) follows from (\textbf{0}, 20.4.7) and (17.15.3, \emph{c)}).

Finally, we have already seen that a) implies b) (17.12.4). Conversely, to prove that b) implies a), we may restrict to the case where $x$ is rational over $k$, by considering, as in the proof of (17.15.3), a point $x'$ of $X'=X\otimes_k k'$ above $x$ such that $\boldsymbol{k}(x')=k'=\boldsymbol{k}(x)$, and using again (17.7.1, (ii)) and the fact that if $X$ is differentially smooth at the point $x$, then $X'$ is differentially smooth at the point $x'$ (16.10.4). Supposing therefore that $x$ is rational over $k$, the fact that $f$ is smooth at $x$ then follows from hypothesis b) and from (17.12.5) applied to the $k$-section $u:\operatorname{Spec}(k)\to X$ such that $u(\operatorname{Spec}(k))=\{x\}$.

\begin{corollary}[17.15.6]
\label{IV.17.15.6}
Let $X$ be a prescheme of finite type over a field $k$. For $X$ to be smooth over $k$, it is necessary and sufficient that the $\sh{O}_X$-Module $\varOmega^1_{X/k}$ be locally free, and that the local rings at the maximal points of $X$ be fields that are separable extensions of $k$ (this last condition being automatically satisfied if $k$ is a perfect field and $X$ a reduced prescheme).
\end{corollary}

The conditions are necessary, for if $X$ is smooth over $k$, it follows from (17.10.2) that $\varOmega^1_{X/k}$ is locally free; on the other hand, $X$ is regular, hence \emph{a fortiori} reduced, so at every maximal point $x$ of $X$, $\sh{O}_{X,x}$ is a field, which must be a formally smooth $k$-algebra for the discrete topologies (17.5.1), hence a separable extension of $k$ (\textbf{0}, 19.6.1).

The conditions are sufficient. Indeed, we may restrict to the case where $X$ is connected, so that $\varOmega^1_{X/k}$ is locally free of constant rank $n$. For every maximal point $x$ of $X$, we then have $\operatorname{rg}_{\kres(x)}\Omega^1_{\kres(x)/k}=n$ since $\sh{O}_{X,x}=\boldsymbol{k}(x)$; since by hypothesis $\boldsymbol{k}(x)$ is a separable extension of $k$, we have $\Upsilon_{\kres(x)/k}=0$ (\textbf{0}, 20.6.3), hence $\deg.\operatorname{tr}_k \boldsymbol{k}(x)=n$ by Cartier's equality (\textbf{0}, 21.7.1). All the irreducible components of $X$ therefore have the same dimension $n$ (5.2.1), and we conclude that $X$ is smooth over $k$ at every point by virtue of the fact that c) implies a) in (17.15.5).

\begin{corollary}[17.15.7]
\label{IV.17.15.7}
If $k$ is of characteristic 0, then, for $X$ to be smooth over $k$ at the point $x$, it is necessary and sufficient that $(\varOmega^1_{X/k})_x$ be a free $\sh{O}_{X,x}$-module.
\end{corollary}

This follows from (16.12.2) and from the equivalence of b) and a) in (17.15.5).

\begin{proposition}[17.15.8]
\label{IV.17.15.8}
Let $k$ be a field, $X$ a prescheme locally of finite type over $k$, and $x$ a point of $X$; set $n=\dim_x(X)$, $r=\dim(\sh{O}_{X,x})$, so that $n\setmin r=\deg.\operatorname{tr}_k \boldsymbol{k}(x)$ \textup{(5.2.3)}. Let $(g_i)_{1\leq i\leq n}$ be a family of $n$ sections of $\sh{O}_X$ over $X$ such that $(g_i)_x\in\mathfrak m_x$ for $1\leq i\leq r$; let $f:X\to Y=\operatorname{Spec}(k[T_1,\ldots,T_n])$ be the morphism corresponding to the $k$-homomorphism $k[T_1,\ldots,T_n]\to\Gamma(X,\sh{O}_X)$ that sends $T_i$ to $g_i$. The following conditions are equivalent (and imply that $X$ is smooth over $k$ at the point $x$ and that $\boldsymbol{k}(x)$ is a separable extension of $k$):
\begin{enumerate}
  \item[\textup{a)}] $f$ is étale at the point $x$.

\begin{proposition}[17.15.5]
\label{IV.17.15.5}
Soient $k$ un corps, $X$ un préschéma localement de type fini sur $k$, $x$ un point de $X$, $n=\dim_x(X)$. Les conditions suivantes sont équivalentes:
\begin{enumerate}
  \item[\textup{a)}] $X$ est lisse sur $k

=================== PAGE IV-4 101 ===================
----- (English page IV-4 100: shown above in this file) -----
----- (English page IV-4 101: shown above in this file) -----
----- English, ega4/ega4-17.tex, page IV-4 102 -----
\oldpage[IV-4]{102}

  \item[\textup{b)}] The $(g_i)_x$ such that $1\leq i\leq r$ generate $\mathfrak m_x$ \textup{(and consequently form a regular system of parameters for $\sh{O}_{X,x}$ (\textbf{0}, 17.1.6))}, and the images in $\Omega^1_{\kres(x)/k}$ of the elements $(d_{X/k}g_j)_x$ for $r+1\leq j\leq n$ generate $\Omega^1_{\kres(x)/k}$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

Indeed, we have (\textbf{0}, 20.5.12.1) the exact sequence of $\boldsymbol{k}(x)$-modules
\[
\label{IV.17.15.8.1}
\tag{17.15.8.1}
\mathfrak m_x/\mathfrak m_x^2\to(\varOmega^1_{X/k})_x\otimes_{\sh{O}_{X,x}}\boldsymbol{k}(x)\to\Omega^1_{\kres(x)/k}\to0
\]
and condition b) consequently implies that the $(d_{X/k}g_i)_x$ generate $(\varOmega^1_{X/k})_x$, taking into account Nakayama's lemma; the fact that b) implies a) therefore follows from (17.15.3). Conversely, if a) holds, the $(d_{X/k}g_i)_x$ for $1\leq i\leq n$ form a basis of $(\varOmega^1_{X/k})_x$ by virtue of (17.15.3). If $t_i$ ($1\leq i\leq n$) is the image of $(g_i)_x$ in $\boldsymbol{k}(x)$, we conclude from the above that the $dt_i$ generate $\Omega^1_{\kres(x)/k}$ for $1\leq i\leq n$, and since by hypothesis $t_i=0$ for $1\leq i\leq r$, the $dt_i$ for $r+1\leq i\leq n$ already generate $\Omega^1_{\kres(x)/k}$. Since $\deg.\operatorname{tr}_k \boldsymbol{k}(x)=n\setmin r$, it follows from Cartier's equality (\textbf{0}, 21.7.1) that $\Upsilon_{\kres(x)/k}=0$, hence $\boldsymbol{k}(x)$ is a separable extension of $k$ (\textbf{0}, 20.6.3), and the sequence of $\boldsymbol{k}(x)$-vector spaces
\[
\label{IV.17.15.8.2}
\tag{17.15.8.2}
0\to\mathfrak m_x/\mathfrak m_x^2\to(\varOmega^1_{X/k})_x\otimes_{\sh{O}_{X,x}}\boldsymbol{k}(x)\to\Omega^1_{\kres(x)/k}\to0
\]
is exact ((\textbf{0}, 20.5.14) and (\textbf{0}, 19.6.1)); moreover, the $dt_i$ for $r+1\leq i\leq n$ form a basis of $\Omega^1_{\kres(x)/k}$, so none of the $t_i$ such that $r+1\leq i\leq n$ can be zero. This shows that the images of the $(g_i)_x$ in $\mathfrak m_x/\mathfrak m_x^2$ for $1\leq i\leq r$ necessarily generate $\mathfrak m_x/\mathfrak m_x^2$, hence the $(g_i)_x$ for $1\leq i\leq r$ generate $\mathfrak m_x$ by virtue of Nakayama's lemma, which completes the proof that a) implies b).

\begin{corollary}[17.15.9]
\label{IV.17.15.9}
Let $X$ be a prescheme locally of finite type over a field $k$, $x$ a point of $X$, and set $n=\dim_x(X)$, $r=\dim(\sh{O}_{X,x})$. The following conditions are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}
  \item[\textup{a)}] The ring $\sh{O}_{X,x}$ is regular and $\boldsymbol{k}(x)$ is a separable extension of $k$.
  \item[\textup{b)}] $X$ is smooth over $k$ at the point $x$, and the canonical homomorphism
  \[
  \mathfrak m_x/\mathfrak m_x^2\to(\varOmega^1_{X/k})_x\otimes_{\sh{O}_{X,x}}\boldsymbol{k}(x)
  \]
  is injective.
  \item[\textup{c)}] There exist $n$ sections $g_i$ of $\sh{O}_X$ over an open neighbourhood $U$ of $x$ such that $(g_i)_x\in\mathfrak m_x$ for $1\leq i\leq r$ and such that the morphism $U\to\operatorname{Spec}(k[T_1,\ldots,T_n])$ corresponding to the $g_i$ (cf. (17.15.8)) is étale at the point $x$.
  \item[\textup{d)}] There exist $n$ sections $g_i$ \textup{(}$1\leq i\leq n$\textup{)} of $\sh{O}_X$ over an open neighbourhood $U$ of $x$ such that the $(g_i)_x$ for $1\leq i\leq r$ generate $\mathfrak m_x$ and the images in $\Omega^1_{\kres(x)/k}$ of the $(d_{U/k}g_j)_x$ for $r+1\leq j\leq n$ generate $\Omega^1_{\kres(x)/k}$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{corollary}

The equivalence of c) and d) follows from (17.15.8). Moreover, we have seen in the proof of (17.15.8) that condition \emph{c)} implies that $X$ is smooth over $k$ at the point $x$ and that the sequence (17.15.8.2) is exact; hence c) implies b). Condition b) implies that the ring $\sh{O}_{X,x}$ is regular, so $\mathfrak m_x/\mathfrak m_x^2$ is of rank $r$; moreover, $(\varOmega^1_{X/k})_x$ is then a free $\sh{O}_{X,x}$-module of rank $n$ (17.10.2); since the sequence (17.15.8.2) is exact by hypothesis, $\Omega^1_{\kres(x)/k}$ is of rank $n\setmin r=\deg.\operatorname{tr}_k \boldsymbol{k}(x)$, and Cartier's equality shows that $\Upsilon_{\kres(x)/k}=0$, so $\boldsymbol{k}(x)$ is separable over $k$ (\textbf{0}, 20.6.3); thus b) implies a). Finally, if a)

\begin{proposition}[17.15.8]
\label{IV.17.15.8}
Soient $k$ un corps, $X$ un préschéma localement de type fini sur $k$, $x$ un point de $X$; posons $n=\dim_x(X)$, $r=\dim(\sh{O}_{X,x})$, de sorte que $n\setmin r=\deg.\operatorname{tr}_k \boldsymbol

=================== PAGE IV-4 102 ===================
----- (English page IV-4 101: shown above in this file) -----
----- (English page IV-4 102: shown above in this file) -----
----- English, ega4/ega4-17.tex, page IV-4 103 -----
\oldpage[IV-4]{103}
holds, we again deduce from Cartier's equality that $\Omega^1_{\kres(x)/k}$ is of rank $n\setmin r$. Since, on the other hand, the ring $\sh{O}_{X,x}$ is regular, the existence of the $g_i$ satisfying the conditions of d) is immediate.

\begin{remarks}[17.15.10]
\label{IV.17.15.10}
\begin{enumerate}
  \item[(i)] For a prescheme $X$ of finite type over $k$ to be smooth over $k$, it does not suffice that $\varOmega^1_{X/k}$ be locally free, as is shown by the example where $X=\operatorname{Spec}(K)$, $K$ being a finite extension of $k$ that is not separable.
  \item[(ii)] When $k$ is not perfect, it can happen that $X$ is smooth over $k$ without $\boldsymbol{k}(x)$ being separable over $k$. We obtain an example by taking $X=\operatorname{Spec}(k[T])$ and for $x$ the point corresponding to the principal prime ideal $(T^p\setmin\lambda)$ ($p>0$ the characteristic of $k$, $\lambda\in k\setmin k^p$).
  \item[(iii)] However, if $X$ is a prescheme smooth over $k$, the set of closed points of $X$ such that $\boldsymbol{k}(x)$ is \emph{separable} (and finite) over $k$ is \emph{dense} in $X$. Indeed, let $f:X\to\operatorname{Spec}(k)$ be the structural morphism; for every $x_0\in X$, there are an open neighbourhood $U$ of $x_0$ in $X$ and a factorization of $f|U:U\xrightarrow{g}\operatorname{Spec}(k[T_1,\ldots,T_n])\xrightarrow{h}\operatorname{Spec}(k)$ where $g$ is \emph{étale} (17.11.4). Since we may restrict to the case where $k$ is not perfect, hence infinite, the set of points of the open subset $g(U)$ that are \emph{rational} over $k$ is non-empty; if $y$ is such a point and $x\in U$ a point above $y$, then $x$ is closed in $X$ and $\boldsymbol{k}(x)$ is separable over $\boldsymbol{k}(y)=k$ (17.6.2).
\end{enumerate}
\end{remarks}

\begin{proposition}[17.15.11]
\label{IV.17.15.11}
Let $X$ be a prescheme of finite type over a field $k$. The following conditions are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}
  \item[\textup{a)}] $X$ is étale over $k$.
  \item[\textup{b)}] $X$ is unramified over $k$.
  \item[\textup{c)}] $X$ is isomorphic to $\operatorname{Spec}(A)$, where $A$ is a finite and separable $k$-algebra.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

This follows from (17.6.2) and (17.4.2), taking into account the fact that condition b) implies here that $X$ is finite over $k$.

\begin{proposition}[17.15.12]
\label{IV.17.15.12}
Let $k$ be a field and $X$ a prescheme locally of finite type over $k$. For there to exist an everywhere dense open subset $U$ of $X$ that is smooth over $k$, it is necessary and sufficient that for every maximal point $x$ of $X$, $X$ be reduced at this point and $\boldsymbol{k}(x)$ be a separable extension of $k$. For there to exist an everywhere dense open subset $U$ of $X$ such that $U_{\mathrm{red}}$ is smooth over $k$, it is necessary and sufficient that for every maximal point $x$ of $X$, $\boldsymbol{k}(x)$ be a separable extension of $k$.
\end{proposition}

The second assertion evidently follows from the first. To say that there exists an everywhere dense open subset $U$ smooth over $k$ means that $X$ is smooth over $k$ at each of its maximal points $x$. For this it is necessary that $\sh{O}_{X,x}$ be regular (17.15.1) and \emph{a fortiori} reduced, hence a field, equal to $\boldsymbol{k}(x)$; moreover (17.15.1), $\boldsymbol{k}(x)\otimes_k k'$ must be regular for every radicial extension $k'$ of $k$, so $\boldsymbol{k}(x)$ must be a separable extension of $k$ (4.3.5); the converse is immediate, by virtue of (17.15.1).

\begin{corollary}[17.15.13]
\label{IV.17.15.13}
Let $X$ be an algebraic prescheme over a field $k$. Then there exist an everywhere dense open subset $U$ in $X$ and a finite radicial extension $k'$ of $k$ such that $(U_{(k')})_{\mathrm{red}}$ is smooth over $k'$.
\end{corollary}

Indeed, there is such an extension $k'$ for which $(X_{(k')})_{\mathrm{red}}$ is geometrically reduced over $k'$ (4.6.6), which is equivalent to saying (4.6.1) that for every maximal point $x'$ of $X_{(k')}$, $\boldsymbol{k}(x')$ is a separable extension of $k'$. We therefore conclude from (17.15.12) that there is an everywhere dense open subset $U'$ of $X_{(k')}$ such that $U'_{\mathrm{red}}$ is smooth over $k'$. But the

\begin{proposition}[17.15.14]
\label{IV.17.15.14}
Let $X$ be an algebraic prescheme over $k$, of dimension $\leq1$. Then there exists a finite radicial extension $k'$ of $k$ such that the normalization \textup{(\textbf{II}, 6.3.8)} of $(X_{(k')})_{\mathrm{red}}$ is smooth over $k'$.
\end{proposition}

Let us first prove the following two lemmas:

\begin{lemma}[17.15.14.1]
\label{IV.17.15.14.1}
Let $X$ be a reduced prescheme whose set of irreducible components is locally finite, and $f:Y\to X$ a morphism. For $Y$ to be $X$-isomorphic to the normalization $X'$ of $X$ \textup{(\textbf{II}, 6.3.8)}, it is necessary and sufficient that the following conditions be satisfied:
\begin{enumerate}
  \item[\textup{(i)}] $Y$ is normal;
  \item[\textup{(ii)}] $f$ is an integral and birational morphism.
\end{enumerate}
When there exists in $X$ a dense and normal open subset (which is always the case when $X$ is excellent \textup{(7.8.3)}, in particular when $X$ is locally of finite type over a field), we may replace condition \textup{(ii)} by the following:
\begin{enumerate}
  \item[\textup{(ii \emph{bis})}] $f$ is integral and there exists a dense open subset $U$ in $X$ such that $f^{-1}(U)$ is dense in $Y$ and the restriction $f^{-1}(U)\to U$ of $f$ is an isomorphism.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

The question being local on $X$, we may suppose that $X$ has only finitely many irreducible components; let $X_i$ ($1\leq i\leq m$) be the reduced sub-preschemes of $X$ having these components as underlying spaces. We know (\textbf{II}, 6.3.6) that $X'$ is the sum of the normalizations $X'_i$ of the $X_i$; each of the structural morphisms $f_i:X'_i\to X_i$ is therefore integral and birational, hence $f$ is integral and birational; it is immediate that (ii) implies (ii \emph{bis}) when there exists a dense and normal open subset $V$ in $X$, by taking $U=V$. Conversely, if conditions (i) and (ii) are fulfilled, $Y$ has only finitely many irreducible components $Y_i$ ($1\leq i\leq m$), $Y_i$ dominating $X_i$ for each index $i$ (6.15.4); $Y$ is the sum of the $Y_i$ since it is normal, and the morphism $g_i:Y_i\to X_i$ through which the restriction $Y_i\to X$ of $f$ factors (\textbf{I}, 5.2.2) is birational; since $X_i$ and $Y_i$ are integral, it then follows from the fact that $g_i$ is integral and $Y_i$ normal that, for every affine open subset $V$ of $X_i$, $\Gamma(g_i^{-1}(V),\sh{O}_{Y_i})$ is the integral closure of $\Gamma(V,\sh{O}_{X_i})$, hence $Y$ is canonically identified with $X'$ (\textbf{II}, 6.3.4). Finally, if we suppose condition (ii \emph{bis}) satisfied, we may restrict to the case where $U$ is the union of disjoint irreducible open subsets $U_i$ ($1\leq i\leq m$), so that $f^{-1}(U)$ is the disjoint union of the $V_i=f^{-1}(U_i)$. Since the $V_i$ are irreducible and $f^{-1}(U)$ is dense in $Y$, the $\overline{V}_i$ are the irreducible components of $Y$, and consequently $f$ is birational.

\begin{lemma}[17.15.14.2]
\label{IV.17.15.14.2}
Let $k$ be a field and $X$ an algebraic prescheme over $k$. Then there exists a finite radicial extension $k'$ of $k$ such that $(X_{(k')})_{\mathrm{red}}$ is geometrically reduced over $k'$ and its normalization is geometrically normal over $k'$.
\end{lemma}

Taking into account (4.6.6) and (\textbf{I}, 5.1.8), we may restrict to the case where $X$ is already geometrically reduced over $k$. Let $p$ be the characteristic exponent of $k$ and $k_1=k^{p^{-\infty}}$ the smallest perfect extension of $k$; $k_1$ is thus the inductive limit of the finite radicial extensions of $k$. Set $X_1=X_{(k_1)}$, which is reduced by hypothesis, and let $Y_1$ be its normalization; if $f_1:Y_1\to X_1$ is the structural morphism, $f_1$ is therefore finite (7.8.3, (vi)) and surjective, and there exists a dense open subset $U_1$ in $X_1$ such that $f_1^{-1}(U_1)=V_1$ is dense in $Y_1$ and

\oldpage[IV-4]{103}
est vérifiée, on déduit encore de l'égalité de Cartier que $\Omega^1_{\mathbf{k}(x)/k}$ est de rang $n\setmin r$. Comme d'autre part l'anneau $\sh{O}_{X,x}$ est régulier, l'existence des $g_i$ vérifiant les conditions de \emph{d)} est immédiate.
